\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r^{2M}}\frac{1}{s^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}[\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\mathcal{L}}_{(1)}^{M}\varphi^{(1)}]_{r}(z,t^{\prime})[\mathcal{L}_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}]_{s}(t-t^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime};

the integrand vanishes off an interval of length
O(r^{2})
because
[\varphi^{(1)}]_{r}
is supported in
B^{(1)}(o,r)
, so

(2.22)

\displaystyle\bigl\|\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\mathcal{L}}_{(1)}^{M}\mathcal{L}_{(2)}\varphi_{r,s}\bigr\|_{\mathsf{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}

\displaystyle\lesssim r^{2-2M}\frac{1}{s^{2}}\bigl\|[\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\mathcal{L}}_{(1)}^{M}\varphi^{(1)}]_{r}\bigr\|_{\mathsf{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\bigl\|(\mathcal{L}_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)})_{s}\bigr\|_{\mathsf{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle=r^{2-2M-\delta}s^{-3}\bigl\|\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\mathcal{L}}_{(1)}^{M}\varphi^{(1)}\bigr\|_{\mathsf{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\bigl\|\mathcal{L}_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}\bigr\|_{\mathsf{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}.

Combining the estimates from (
2.21
) to (
2.22
), we deduce that

(2.23)

\left|a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}\right|^{2}\ast_{(1)}\chi_{r,s}\lesssim_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}r^{-4M-4\nu}s^{-6}h^{5}\left\|a_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{T(o,2r+1,2s+1)}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}\int_{h}^{\infty}\bigl|a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}\bigr|^{2}\ast_{(1)}\chi_{r,s}\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\frac{dr}{r}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\biggl(\sum_{i,j\in\mathbb{N}}\int_{\alpha^{i}}^{\alpha^{i+1}}\int_{\alpha^{2j}h}^{\alpha^{2(j+1)}h}\left\|\left|a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}\right|^{2}\ast_{(1)}\chi_{r,s}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\mathbbm{1}_{T(o,2r+1,2s+h)}\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\frac{dr}{r}\biggr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq h^{-1/2}\left\|a_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\biggl(\sum_{i,j\in\mathbb{N}}\alpha^{-4(M+\nu)i-12j}\mathbbm{1}_{T(o,3\alpha^{i+1},3\alpha^{2(j+1)}h)}\biggr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq h^{-1/2}\left\|a_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\sum_{i,j\in\mathbb{N}}\alpha^{-2(M+\nu)i-6j}\mathbbm{1}_{T(o,3\alpha^{i+1},3\alpha^{2(j+1)}h)}.

Clearly
\left|T(o,3\alpha^{i+1},3\alpha^{2(j+1)}h)\right|\eqsim\alpha^{2\nu i}(\alpha^{2i}+\alpha^{2j}h)
.
If
(z,t)\in T(o,3\alpha^{i+1},3\alpha^{2(j+1)}h)
, where
r\geq 1
and
s\geq h
, then
|z|\leq 3\alpha^{i+1}
and
|t|\leq 9\alpha^{2(i+1)}+3\alpha^{2(j+1)}h
;
we write
I(r^{*},s^{*})
for the set of all
(i,j)\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
for which
r^{*}\leq 3\alpha^{i+1}
or
s^{*}\leq 9\alpha^{2(i+1)}+3\alpha^{2(j+1)}h
; this last inequality implies that
s^{*}\leq 18\alpha^{2(i+1)}
or
s^{*}\leq 6\alpha^{2(j+1)}h
.
We conclude that if
(i,j)\in I(r^{*},s^{*})
, then
\alpha^{i}\geq r^{*}/3\alpha
or
\alpha^{2i}\geq s^{*}/18\alpha^{2}
or
\alpha^{2j}\geq s^{*}/6\alpha^{2}h
.
Thus

(2.24)

\displaystyle\int_{(S^{*})^{c}}\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}^{4}(a_{R})(g)\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}h^{-1/2}\left\|a_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\sum_{(i,j)\in I(r^{*},s^{*})}\frac{\alpha^{2i}+\alpha^{2j}h}{\alpha^{2Mi+6j}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}h^{-1/2}\left\|a_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\biggl(\sum_{i:\alpha^{i}\geq r^{*}/3\alpha}\frac{\alpha^{2i}+h}{\alpha^{2Mi}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i:\alpha^{2i}\geq s^{*}/18\alpha^{2}}\frac{\alpha^{2i}+h}{\alpha^{2Mi}}+\sum_{j:\alpha^{2j}\geq s^{*}/9\alpha^{2}}\frac{1+\alpha^{2j}h}{\alpha^{6j}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}h^{-1/2}\left\|a_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\biggl(\frac{(r^{*})^{2}+h}{(r^{*})^{2M}}+\frac{s^{*}+h}{(s^{*})^{M}}+\frac{h}{(s^{*})^{2}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{1}{r^{*}}\right)^{2(M-1)}\left|R\right|^{1/2}\left\|a_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}+\left(\frac{h}{s^{*}}\right)^{2}\left|R\right|^{1/2}\left\|a_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}.

We require that
M>1
to ensure that the sums above converge, whence
2(M-1)\geq 2
.

The quotients preceding
\left|R\right|^{1/2}\left\|a_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}
on the right hand sides of (
2.17
), (
2.19
) and (
2.24
) are dominated by
\rho_{1,3/2}(R,S)
, as defined in (
1.23
).
Further, examination of the argument shows that the implicit constants depend on
\boldsymbol{\varphi}
through products such as
\bigl\|\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\mathcal{L}}_{(1)}^{M}\varphi^{(1)}\bigr\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\bigl\|\mathcal{L}_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}\bigr\|_{\mathsf{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}
, which, because of the support restriction, may all be controlled by the product
\left\|\mathcal{R}\varphi^{(1)}\right\|_{(2M)}\left\|\varphi^{(2)}\right\|_{(2)}
.
∎

Before the next corollary, we recall that
h
and
p
denote the heat and Poisson kernels.

Corollary 2.18
.

Suppose that
\varphi^{(1)}
and
\varphi^{(2)}
are either
\mathcal{L}_{(1)}h^{(1)}_{1}
and
\mathcal{L}_{(2)}h^{(2)}_{1}
or
\mathcal{L}_{(1)}p^{(1)}_{1}
and
\mathcal{L}_{(2)}p^{(2)}_{1}
on
\mathbb{H}^{\nu}
and on
\mathbb{R}
, and that
\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)
is as in (
2.12
).
Then
\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
and

\bigl\|\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\bigr\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\qquad\forall f\in\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu}).

Proof.

This result follows from the theorem above and the estimates for the heat and Poisson kernels in Section
1.6
.
∎

Corollary 2.19
.

Suppose that
\boldsymbol{\varphi}
is a Poisson bounded pair of functions, both of which have mean
0
.
Then, if
\theta\in(0,1)
,

\left(\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}\sup_{g\in T(o,\theta r,\theta s)}\left|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(\cdot g)\right|^{2}\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\frac{ds}{s}\right)^{1/2}\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})

for all
f\in\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, and a corresponding norm inequality holds.

Proof.

Define
\psi^{(1)}=\mathcal{L}_{(1)}^{M}\varphi^{(1)}
and
\psi^{(2)}=\mathcal{L}_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}
, where
M>\delta/4
.
In light of Theorem
2.17
, the area function
\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)
is in
\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, as are the area functions associated to the pairs
(\varphi^{(1)},\psi^{(2)})
,
(\psi^{(1)},\varphi^{(2)})
and
(\psi^{(1)},\psi^{(2)})
.
Hence by the flag Sobolev inequalities of Lemma
1.20
,

\displaystyle\biggl(\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}\sup_{g^{\prime}\in T(g,r,s)}\left|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g^{\prime})\right|^{2}\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\frac{ds}{s}\biggr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\biggl(\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}\frac{1}{|T(o,r,s)|}\int_{T(g,r,s)}\left|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)}_{r}\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{s}(g^{\prime})\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+\left|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)}_{r}\ast_{(2)}\psi^{(2)}_{s}(g^{\prime})\right|^{2}+\left|f\ast_{(1)}\psi^{(1)}_{r}\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{s}(g^{\prime})\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+\left|f\ast_{(1)}\psi^{(1)}_{r}\ast_{(2)}\psi^{(2)}_{s}(g^{\prime})\right|^{2}\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\frac{ds}{s}\biggr)^{1/2},

and the right hand side function is in
\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, as claimed.
∎

2.6.
The inclusion
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\subseteq\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{cts}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\cap\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{dis}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})

Now we treat the square function Hardy spaces.
We begin with the definitions.

Definition
.

Suppose that
\boldsymbol{\varphi}
is a w-invertible, Poisson bounded pair of functions, both of which have mean
0
and whose w-inverses have mean
0
.
For
f\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, we define the
continuous Littlewood–Paley square function
\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{cts},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)
associated to
\boldsymbol{\varphi}
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{cts},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)(g):=\biggl(\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}\bigl|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)}_{r}\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{s}(g)\bigr|^{2}\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\frac{ds}{s}\biggr)^{1/2}

for all
g\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu}
.
We define the continuous square function Hardy space
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{cts},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, often written
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{cts}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, to be the set of all
f\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
for which
\left\|\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{cts},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}
is finite, with norm

\displaystyle\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{cts},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}

\displaystyle:=\left\|\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{cts},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}.

Definition
.

Suppose that
\varphi^{(1)}
and
\varphi^{(2)}
are as above.
For
f\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, we define the
discrete Littlewood–Paley square function

\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{dis},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)
associated to
\varphi^{(1)}
and
\varphi^{(2)}
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{dis},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)(g):=\biggl(\sum_{(m,n)\in\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}}\bigl|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)}_{2^{m}}\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{2^{n}}(g)\bigr|^{2}\biggr)^{1/2}

for all
g\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu}
.
We define the
discrete square function Hardy space

\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{dis},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, often written
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{dis}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, to be the set of all
f\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
for which
\left\|\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{dis},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}
is finite, with norm

\displaystyle\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{dis},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}

\displaystyle:=\left\|\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{dis},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}.

Theorem 2.20
.

Suppose that the functions
\varphi^{(1)}
and
\varphi^{(2)}
on
\mathbb{H}^{\nu}
and
\mathbb{R}
are as above.
Then the operators
\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{cts},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{dis},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}
are bounded from
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
to
\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
.

Proof.

From Corollary
2.19
,
\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{cts},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
and
\left\|\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{cts},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}
.
By Lemma
1.47
,
\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{dis},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
and
\left\|\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{dis},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}
.
∎

We may also prove this theorem directly using simplified versions of the proof of Theorem
2.17
and Corollary
1.31
to ensure that
\varphi^{(1)}
and
\varphi^{(2)}
have sufficiently many vanishing moments.

2.7.
The inclusion
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\subseteq\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{gmax}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})

We recall the key points of Definition
1.26
.
A family
\mathsf{F}
of pairs of functions
(\varphi^{(1)},\varphi^{(2)})
,
where
\varphi^{(1)}:\mathbb{H}^{\nu}\to\mathbb{C}
and
\varphi^{(2)}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{C}
, is said to be
Poisson-bounded
if there are constants
C_{m}
and
C_{n}
for all
m,n\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{D}\varphi^{(1)}(g)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{m}}{(1+\left\|g\right\|)^{m+\delta+1}}\qquad\forall g\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu}

for all differential operators
\mathcal{D}
that are products of
m
vector fields, each chosen from
\mathcal{X}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{X}_{2\nu}
, and

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{T}^{n}\varphi^{(2)}(t)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{n}}{(1+\left\|t\right\|)^{n+2}}\qquad\forall t\in\mathbb{R}

for all pairs
(\varphi^{(1)},\varphi^{(2)})\in\mathsf{F}
.

Definition 2.21
.

The
grand maximal function

\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}}(f)
of
f\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, for a Poisson bounded family
\mathsf{F}
, is defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}}(f)(g):=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}\in\mathsf{F}}\sup_{r,s\in\mathbb{R}^{+}}\left|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g)\right|\qquad\forall g\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu}.

The space
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{gmax}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
is defined to be the linear space of all
f\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
such that
\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}}(f)\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, with norm

(2.25)

\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{gmax}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}:=\left\|\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}.

Theorem 2.22
.

Suppose that
\mathsf{F}
is Poisson bounded.
Then
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\subseteq\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, and

\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\lesssim_{\mathsf{F}}\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\qquad\forall f\in\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu}).

Proof.

The proof resembles that of Theorem
2.17
.
Let
M
be the smallest integer such that
2M>\delta/2
.

By Remark
1.33
(or the preceding result), it is enough to consider a Poisson bounded family
\mathsf{F}_{0}
of functions
\varphi^{(1)}
and
\varphi^{(2)}
that are supported in the unit balls of
\mathbb{H}^{\nu}
and
\mathbb{R}
.
Next, by Proposition
2.16
, it suffices to show that

(2.26)

\displaystyle\int_{(S^{*})^{c}}\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}_{0}}(a_{R})(g)\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}\in\mathsf{F}_{0}}\bigl\|\mathcal{R}\varphi^{(1)}\bigr\|_{(2M)}\bigl\|\varphi^{(2)}\bigr\|_{(2)}\rho_{1,3/2}(R,S)\left|R\right|^{1/2}\left\|a_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}

for all particles
a_{R}
associated to a shard
R
such that
\operatorname{c}(R)=o
,
\operatorname{w}(R)=1
and
\operatorname{h}(R)=h
and all shards
S
such that
\operatorname{c}(S)=o
,
\operatorname{w}(S)=r^{*}>1
and
\operatorname{h}(S)=s^{*}>h
, where
\rho_{1,3/2}
is as defined in (
1.23
).

From the definition just before the statement of this theorem,

\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}_{0}}(a_{R})\eqsim\max_{j=1,\dots,4}\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}_{0}}^{j}(a_{R}),

where

\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}_{0}}^{j}(a_{R})(g)=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}\in\mathsf{F}_{0}}\sup_{(r,s)\in\Omega_{j}}\left|a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g)\right|,

and the regions
\Omega^{j}
, which partition
\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
into four, are defined by

\displaystyle\Omega_{1}

\displaystyle=\{(r,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}:r<1,s<h\}\qquad

\displaystyle\Omega_{2}

\displaystyle=\{(r,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}:r<1,s>h\}

\displaystyle\Omega_{3}

\displaystyle=\{(r,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}:r>1,s<h\}

\displaystyle\Omega_{4}

\displaystyle=\{(r,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}:r>1,s>h\}.

We treat these four summands separately.
The key to our estimation is the fact that for all
\boldsymbol{\varphi}
in
\mathsf{F}_{0}
,

(2.27)

\displaystyle\operatorname{supp}\bigl(a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}\bigr)\subseteq T(o,1,h)T(o,r,s)=T(o,r+1,s+h).

First, (
2.27
) implies that

\operatorname{supp}(\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}_{0}}^{1}(a_{R}))\cap(S^{*})^{c}=\emptyset
and so no estimation is needed.

Second, to treat
\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}_{0}}^{2}(a_{R})
and
\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}_{0}}^{3}(a_{R})
, we first choose an exponentially increasing family of sets
E_{j}
such that, from (
2.27
),

\operatorname{supp}(\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}_{0}}^{k}(a_{R}))\subseteq\bigcup_{(r,s)\in\Omega_{k}}T(o,1+r,h+s)\subseteq\bigcup_{j\in\mathbb{N}}E_{j}.

We then take
j^{*}
to be the smallest
j
such that
E_{j}\cap(S^{*})^{c}\neq\emptyset
and
\Omega_{k,j}
to be a subset of
\Omega_{k}
such that
(r,s)\notin\Omega_{k.j}
implies that
T(o,1+r,h+s)\subseteq E_{j}
.
Then, by the definitions and basic results,

(2.28)

\displaystyle\int_{(S^{*})^{c}}|\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}_{0}}^{k}(a_{R})(g)|\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geq j^{*}}\int_{E_{j+1}\setminus E_{j}}|\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}_{0}}^{k}(a_{R}))(g)|\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\geq j^{*}}\left|E_{j+1}\setminus E_{j}\right|^{1/2}\left(\int_{(E_{j})^{c}}|\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}_{0}}^{k}(a_{R}))(g)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}g\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geq j^{*}}\left|E_{j+1}\setminus E_{j}\right|^{1/2}\left(\int_{(E_{j})^{c}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}\in\mathsf{F}_{0}}\sup_{(r,s)\in\Omega_{k}}\left|a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g)\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}g\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geq j^{*}}\left|E_{j+1}\setminus E_{j}\right|^{1/2}\left(\int_{(E_{j})^{c}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}\in\mathsf{F}_{0}}\sup_{(r,s)\in\Omega_{k,j}}\left|a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g)\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}g\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\geq j^{*}}\left|E_{j+1}\right|^{1/2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}\in\mathsf{F}_{0}}\sup_{(r,s)\in\Omega_{k,j}}\left|a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g)\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}g\right)^{1/2}.

To treat
\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}_{0}}^{2}(a_{R})
, we define
E_{j}:=T(o,2,2\times 2^{j}h)
and
\Omega_{k,j}:=(0,1)\times(2^{j}h,\infty)
when
j\in\mathbb{N}
; then
\left|E_{j}\right|\eqsim 2^{j}h
and
h^{*}\eqsim\log_{2}(s^{*})
.
Moreover,

\displaystyle a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}

\displaystyle=s^{-2}\mathcal{L}_{(1)}^{M}b_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)}_{r}\ast_{(2)}(\mathcal{L}_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)})_{s};

see (
2.16
).
We define the family
{\mathsf{F}_{1}}
to be
\{(\varphi^{(1)},\mathcal{L}_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}):(\varphi^{(1)},\varphi^{(2)})\in\mathsf{F}_{0}\}
.
Then

(2.29)

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}\in\mathsf{F}_{0}}\sup_{(r,s)\in\Omega_{k,j}}\left|a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}\right|

\displaystyle\leq(2^{j}h)^{-2}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}\in\mathsf{F}_{0}}\sup_{(r,s)\in\Omega_{k,j}}\left|\mathcal{L}_{(1)}^{M}b_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)}_{r}\ast_{(2)}(\mathcal{L}_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)})_{s}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim(2^{j}h)^{-2}\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}_{1}}(\mathcal{L}_{(1)}^{M}b_{R}).

From (
2.28
), (
2.29
), and Lemmas
1.29
,
1.11
, and
2.2
,

(2.30)

\displaystyle\int_{(S^{*})^{c}}|\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}}^{k}(a_{R})(g)|\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\geq j^{*}}\left|E_{j+1}\right|^{1/2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}\in\mathsf{F}_{0}}\sup_{(r,s)\in\Omega_{k,j}}\left|a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g)\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}g\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j\geq j^{*}}\left|E_{j+1}\right|^{1/2}(2^{j}h)^{-2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}}\left|\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}_{1}}(\mathcal{L}_{(1)}^{M}b_{R})(g)\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}g\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j\geq j^{*}}(2^{j}h)^{-3/2}\left\|\mathcal{M}_{F}(\mathcal{L}_{(1)}^{M}b_{R})\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-3j^{*}/2}h^{-3/2}\left\|\mathcal{L}_{(1)}^{M}b_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigl(\frac{h}{s^{*}}\Bigr)^{3/2}\left|R\right|^{1/2}\left\|a_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}^{2}.

To deal with
\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}_{0}}^{3}(a_{R})
, we define
E_{j}:=T(o,1+2r,2h)
and
\Omega_{3,j}:=(2^{j},\infty)\times(0,h)
when
j\in\mathbb{N}
; then
\left|E_{j}\right|\lesssim 2^{\delta j}+2^{2\nu j}h
and
j^{*}\eqsim\log_{2}(r^{*})
.
Further, from (
2.18
),

\displaystyle a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r^{2M}}\mathcal{L}_{(2)}b_{R}\ast_{(1)}[\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\mathcal{L}}_{(1)}^{M}\varphi^{(1)}]_{r}\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{s};

we let
\mathsf{F}_{2}
be the family
\{(\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\mathcal{L}}_{(1)}^{M}\varphi^{(1)},\varphi^{(2)}):(\varphi^{(1)},\varphi^{(2)})\in\mathsf{F}_{0}\}
.
Much as for the proof of (
2.30
),

(2.31)

\displaystyle\int_{(S^{*})^{c}}|\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}_{0}}^{3}(a_{R})(g)|\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j\geq j^{*}}\left|E_{j+1}\right|^{1/2}2^{-2Mj}\left(\int_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}}\left|\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}_{2}}(\mathcal{L}_{(2)}b_{R})(g)\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}g\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j\geq j^{*}}(2^{\delta j}+2^{2\nu j}h)^{1/2}2^{-2Mj}\left\|\mathcal{M}_{F}(\mathcal{L}_{(2)}b_{R})\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigl(\frac{1}{r^{*}}\Bigr)^{2M-\delta/2}\left|R\right|^{1/2}\left\|a_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}^{2}.

Finally, to treat
\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}}^{4}(a_{R})
, we use the pointwise estimate

\left|a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}\right|\lesssim_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}r^{-2M-2\nu}s^{-3}h^{5/2}\left\|a_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\mathbbm{1}_{T(o,r+1,s+1)},

which is proved in a very similar but easier way to (
2.23
).

It follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{(r,s)\in\Omega_{4}}\bigl|a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}\bigr|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sum_{i,j\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{\alpha^{i}<r<\alpha^{i+1}}\sup_{\alpha^{2j}h<s<\alpha^{2(j+1)}h}\left\|a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\mathbbm{1}_{T(o,2r+1,2s+h)}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq h^{-1/2}\left\|a_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\sum_{i,j\in\mathbb{N}}\alpha^{-2(M+\nu)i-6j}\mathbbm{1}_{T(o,3\alpha^{i+1},3\alpha^{2(j+1)}h)}.

In the proof of Theorem
2.17
, we showed that this last function is in
\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, and estimated its norm.
From this it follows that

(2.32)

\displaystyle\int_{(S^{*})^{c}}\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}}^{4}(a_{R})(g)\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{1}{r^{*}}\right)^{2(M-1)}\left|R\right|^{1/2}\left\|a_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}+\left(\frac{h}{s^{*}}\right)^{2}\left|R\right|^{1/2}\left\|a_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}.

The quotients that precede
|R|^{1/2}
on the right hand sides of our estimates are all dominated by multiples of
\rho_{1,3/2}(R,S)
, as defined in (
1.23
).
A careful examination of the proof shows that
\boldsymbol{\varphi}
comes into the constants in expressions involving norms such as

\bigl\|\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\mathcal{L}}_{(1)}^{M}\varphi^{(1)}\bigr\|_{\mathsf{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\bigl\|\mathcal{L}_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}\bigr\|_{\mathsf{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}
or
\bigl\|\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\mathcal{L}}_{(1)}^{M}\varphi^{(1)}\bigr\|_{\mathsf{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\bigl\|\varphi^{(2)}\bigr\|_{\mathsf{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}
,
all of which are dominated by

\bigl\|\mathcal{R}\varphi^{(1)}\bigr\|_{(2M)}\bigl\|\varphi^{(2)}\bigr\|_{(2)}

because of the support restriction.
∎

2.8.
The inclusion
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\subseteq\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{singint},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})

In this section, we consider a homogeneous flag singular integral operator
\mathcal{K}
acting on the atomic Hardy space.

Theorem 2.23
.

Suppose that
\varphi^{(1)}
and
\varphi^{(2)}
are Poisson bounded on
\mathbb{H}^{\nu}
and on
\mathbb{R}
and have mean
0
.
Then
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\subseteq\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{singint},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, and

\left\|\mathcal{K}_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}f\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\qquad\forall f\in\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu}).

Proof.

From Remark
1.33
, we may suppose that
\operatorname{supp}(\varphi^{(1)})\subseteq B^{(1)}(o,1)
and
\operatorname{supp}(\varphi^{(2)})\subseteq B^{(2)}(0,1)
.
The kernel
k
of
\mathcal{K}_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}
is given by

k=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}\varphi^{(1)}_{s}\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{t}\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\frac{dt}{t}\,.

By Proposition
2.16
, it suffices to study
\mathcal{K}_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(a_{R})
, where
a_{R}
is a particle, and to show that

\int_{(S^{*})^{c}}\left|a_{r}\ast_{(1)}k(g)\right|\,dg\lesssim_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}\rho_{2,2}(R,S)\left|R\right|^{1/2}\left\|a_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(G)}

for all shards
S
that contain
R
; here
\rho_{2,2}
is as defined in (
1.23
).

We suppose that
a_{R}=\mathcal{L}_{(1)}\mathcal{L}_{(2)}b_{R}
, where
b_{R}
is supported in the shard
R
, and, by translation and dilation invariance, that
R
is the shard with centre
o
, width
1
and height
h>1
; we may also suppose that
\operatorname{c}(S)=o
,
\operatorname{w}(S)=w^{*}>1
and
\operatorname{h}(S)=h^{*}>h
.

Again, we define four regions
\Omega^{j}
, which partition
\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
, by

\displaystyle\Omega_{1}

\displaystyle=\{(r,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}:r\leq 1,s\leq h\}\qquad

\displaystyle\Omega_{2}

\displaystyle=\{(r,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}:r\leq 1,s>h\}

\displaystyle\Omega_{3}

\displaystyle=\{(r,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}:r>1,s\leq h\}

\displaystyle\Omega_{4}

\displaystyle=\{(r,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}:r>1,s>h\},

and we write
a_{R}\ast_{(1)}k=a_{R}\ast_{(1)}k^{1}+a_{R}\ast_{(1)}k^{2}+a_{R}\ast_{(1)}k^{2}+a_{R}\ast_{(1)}k^{4}
, where

k^{i}:=\iint_{\Omega^{i}}\varphi^{(1)}_{s}\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{t}\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\frac{dt}{t}\,.

We treat the four summands
a_{R}\ast_{(1)}k^{i}
separately.
The key to our estimation is the fact that

(2.33)

\displaystyle\operatorname{supp}\bigl(a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}\bigr)\subseteq T(o,1,h)T(o,r,s)=T(o,r+1,s+h).

First, by definition,
\operatorname{supp}(a_{R}\ast_{(1)}k^{1})\subseteq S
, and there is nothing to consider; in any case,
a_{R}\ast_{(1)}k^{1}\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(G)
.

Second, to treat
a_{R}\ast_{(1)}k^{2}
, we observe that if
a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)}_{s}\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{t}(g)\neq 0
, where
g\in(S)^{c}
and
(s,t)\in\Omega^{2}
, then
h^{*}\leq h+s+1\leq h+2
, and so

\displaystyle\int_{(S)^{c}}\left|a_{R}\ast_{(1)}k^{2}(g)\right|\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle=\int_{(S)^{c}}\left|\int_{h^{*}/2}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{1}a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)}_{s}\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{t}\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\frac{dt}{t}\right|\,\mathrm{d}g.

Recall that
a_{R}=\mathcal{L}_{(2)}\mathcal{L}_{(1)}b_{R}
, and write, for
g\in(S)^{c}
,

\displaystyle a_{R}\ast_{(1)}k^{2}(g)

\displaystyle=\int_{h^{*}/2}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{1}(\mathcal{L}_{(2)}\mathcal{L}_{(1)}b_{R})\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)}_{s}\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{t}\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{h^{*}/2}^{\infty}\left(\int_{0}^{1}(\mathcal{L}_{(1)}b_{R})\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)}_{s}\,\frac{ds}{s}\right)\ast_{(2)}(\mathcal{L}_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)})_{t}\,\frac{dt}{t^{3}}

\displaystyle=\int_{h^{*}/2}^{\infty}c^{2}_{R}\ast_{(2)}(\mathcal{L}_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)})_{t}\,\frac{dt}{t^{3}},

say, where

c^{2}_{R}=\int_{0}^{1}(\mathcal{L}_{(1)}b_{R})\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)}_{s}\,\frac{ds}{s}.

Now
b_{R}
is supported in
R
, so
c^{2}_{R}
is supported in
R^{*}
, say, where
\left|R^{*}\right|\eqsim\left|R\right|
.
Moreover, by Hölder’s inequality and Lemmas
1.40
and
1.19
,

\left\|c^{2}_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\lesssim|R|^{1/2}\left\|c^{2}_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\lesssim_{\varphi^{(1)}}|R|^{1/2}\left\|\mathcal{L}_{(1)}b_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\lesssim h^{2}|R|^{1/2}\left\|a_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}.

We conclude that

\displaystyle\int_{(S)^{c}}\left|a_{R}\ast_{(1)}k^{2}(g)\right|\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle=\int_{(S)^{c}}\left|\int_{h^{*}/2}^{\infty}c^{2}_{R}\ast_{(2)}(\mathcal{L}_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)})_{t}(g)\,\frac{dt}{t^{3}}\right|\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle\leq\int_{h^{*}/2}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}}\left|c^{2}_{R}\ast_{(2)}(\mathcal{L}_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)})_{t}(g)\right|\,\mathrm{d}g\,\frac{dt}{t^{3}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{h^{*}/2}^{\infty}\left\|c^{2}_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\left\|(\mathcal{L}_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)})_{t}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{R})}\,\frac{dt}{t^{3}}

\displaystyle\eqsim_{\varphi^{(2)}}\frac{1}{(h^{*})^{2}}\left\|c^{2}_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\varphi^{(1)}}\frac{h^{2}}{(h^{*})^{2}}\left|R\right|^{1/2}\left\|a_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}.

Third, to treat
a_{R}\ast_{(1)}k^{3}
, we argue similarly.
First, if
a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)}_{s}\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{t}(g)\neq 0
, where
g\in(S)^{c}
and
(s,t)\in\Omega^{3}
, then
w^{*}\leq s+1
, whence

\displaystyle\int_{(S)^{c}}\left|a_{R}\ast_{(1)}k^{3}(g)\right|\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle=\int_{(S)^{c}}\left|\int_{w^{*}/2}^{\infty}\int_{h}^{\infty}a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)}_{s}\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{t}\,\frac{dt}{t}\,\frac{ds}{s}\right|\,\mathrm{d}g.

The next step is to write, for
g\in(S)^{c}
,

\displaystyle a_{R}\ast_{(1)}k^{3}(g)

\displaystyle=\int_{w^{*}/2}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{h}(\mathcal{L}_{(1)}\mathcal{L}_{(2)}b_{R})\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{t}\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)}_{s}(g)\,\frac{dt}{t}\,\frac{ds}{s}

\displaystyle=\int_{w^{*}/2}^{\infty}c^{3}_{R}\ast_{(1)}(\mathcal{L}_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)})_{s}(g)\,\frac{ds}{s^{3}},

say, where

c^{3}_{R}=\int_{0}^{h}(\mathcal{L}_{(2)}b_{R})\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{t}\,\frac{dt}{t}\,.

Finally,

\displaystyle\int_{(S)^{c}}\left|a_{R}\ast_{(1)}k^{3}(g)\right|\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle=\int_{(S)^{c}}\left|\int_{w^{*}/2}^{\infty}c^{3}_{R}\ast_{(1)}(\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\mathcal{L}}_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)})_{s}(g)\,\frac{ds}{s^{3}}\right|\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle\leq\int_{w^{*}/2}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}}\left|c^{3}_{R}\ast_{(1)}(\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\mathcal{L}}_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)})_{s}(g)\right|\,\mathrm{d}g\,\frac{ds}{s^{3}}

\displaystyle\eqsim_{\varphi^{(1)}}\frac{1}{(w^{*})^{2}}\left\|c^{3}_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\varphi^{(2)}}\frac{1}{(w^{*})^{2}}\left|R\right|^{1/2}\left\|a_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}.

Fourth, we treat
a_{R}\ast_{(1)}k^{4}
.
If
a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)}_{s}\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{t}(g)\neq 0
, where
g\in(S)^{c}
and
(s,t)\in\Omega^{4}
, then either
s+1\geq w^{*}
, or
s+1<w^{*}
and
t+s^{2}+h\geq h^{*}
, whence

\displaystyle\int_{(S)^{c}}\left|a_{R}\ast_{(1)}k^{4}(g)\right|\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle\leq\int_{(S)^{c}}\int_{w^{*}/2}^{\infty}\int_{h}^{\infty}\left|a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)}_{s}\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{t}(g)\right|\,\frac{dt}{t}\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle+\int_{(S)^{c}}\int_{1}^{w^{*}}\int_{h^{*}}^{\infty}\left|a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)}_{s}\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{t}(g)\right|\,\frac{dt}{t}\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\mathrm{d}g.

Next,

\displaystyle a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)}_{s}\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{s^{2}t^{2}}b_{R}\ast_{(1)}(\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\mathcal{L}}_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)})_{s}\ast_{(2)}(\mathcal{L}_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)})_{t}.

Finally, estimating much as before, we see that

\displaystyle\int_{(S)^{c}}\int_{w^{*}/2}^{\infty}\int_{h}^{\infty}\left|a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)}_{s}\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{t}(g)\right|\,\frac{dt}{t}\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\int_{w^{*}/2}^{\infty}\int_{h}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}}\left|b_{R}\ast_{(1)}(\mathcal{L}_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)})_{s}\ast_{(2)}(\mathcal{L}_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)})_{t}(g)\right|\,\mathrm{d}g\,\frac{dt}{t^{3}}\,\frac{ds}{s^{3}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\int_{w^{*}/2}^{\infty}\int_{h}^{\infty}\left\|b_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\left\|(\mathcal{L}_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)})_{s}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\left\|(\mathcal{L}_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)})_{t}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{R})}\,\frac{dt}{t^{3}}\,\frac{ds}{s^{3}}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}\frac{1}{h^{2}(w^{*})^{2}}\left\|b_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\frac{1}{(w^{*})^{2}}\left|R\right|^{1/2}\left\|a_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})},

while

\displaystyle\int_{(S)^{c}}\int_{1}^{w^{*}}\int_{h^{*}/2}^{\infty}\left|a_{R}\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)}_{s}\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{t}(g)\right|\,\frac{dt}{t}\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle\leq\int_{1}^{\infty}\int_{h^{*}/2}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}}\left|b_{R}\ast_{(1)}(\mathcal{L}_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)})_{s}\ast_{(2)}(\mathcal{L}_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)})_{t}(g)\right|\,\mathrm{d}g\,\frac{dt}{t^{3}}\,\frac{ds}{s^{3}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{1}^{\infty}\int_{h^{*}/2}^{\infty}\left\|b_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\left\|(\mathcal{L}_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)})_{s}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\left\|(\mathcal{L}_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)})_{t}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{R})}\,\frac{dt}{t^{3}}\,\frac{ds}{s^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}\frac{1}{(h^{*})^{2}}\left\|b_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{h^{2}}{(h^{*})^{2}}\left|R\right|^{1/2}\left\|a_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}.

In conclusion,

\int_{(S^{*})^{c}}\left|a_{r}\ast_{(1)}k(g)\right|\,dg\lesssim_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}\rho_{2,2}(R,S)\left|R\right|^{1/2}\left\|a_{R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(G)},

as required.
∎

We note that the argument of the proof above may be improved to show that
\mathcal{K}_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}
sends particles to
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
-functions.
However, it appears to be nontrivial to then deduce that these operators also send atoms to
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
-functions.
Nevertheless, this conclusion follows from our later characterisation of the Hardy space using Riesz transforms.

We also observe that straightforward modifications of the proof will deal with convolutions with distributions of the form
\sum_{j,k\in\mathbb{Z}}\varphi^{(1)}_{2^{j}}\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(2)}_{2^{k}}
, or more generally with convolutions with distributions of the form
\sum_{j,k\in\mathbb{Z}}\varphi^{(1,j)}_{2^{j}}\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(2,k)}_{2^{k}}
, where the
\{\varphi^{(1,j)}:j\in\mathbb{Z}\}
is a uniformly bounded family of functions with mean
0
in
\mathsf{C}^{\infty}(B^{(1)}(o,1))
and
\{\varphi^{(2,k)}:k\in\mathbb{Z}\}
is a uniformly bounded family of functions with mean
0
in
\mathsf{C}^{\infty}(B^{(2)}(0,1))
.

2.9.
Further remarks

The results of Sections
2.5
to
2.8
may easily be formulated and proved for the moment atomic Hardy space of Section
2.3
.

3.
The Lusin–Littlewood–Paley area function

In this section, we consider the Hardy space defined using area functions.
We first examine the definition and properties of the space, and then show that if
f\in\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, and
\varphi^{(1)}
and
\varphi^{(2)}
satisfy appropriate invertibility conditions, then
f\in\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
.

3.1.
Properties of the area function Hardy space

We show that the space is independent of some of the parameters used in its definition, and prove that the area operator (when suitably normalised) is an isometry on
\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
.

We begin by introducing three cones that we shall discuss.

Definition 3.1
.

Suppose that
g\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu}
and
\beta,\gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
.
The cones
\Gamma_{\beta,\gamma}(g)
,
\Gamma^{(1)}_{\beta}(g)
, and
\Gamma^{(2)}_{\beta}(g)
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\beta,\gamma}(g)

\displaystyle:=\{(g^{\prime},r,s)\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}:g^{\prime}\in T(g,\beta r,\gamma s)\},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(1)}_{\beta}(g)

\displaystyle:=\{(g^{\prime},r)\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}:g^{\prime}\in gB^{(1)}(o,\beta r)\},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(2)}_{\gamma}(g^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle:=\{(g^{\prime\prime\prime},s)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}:g^{\prime\prime\prime}\in g^{\prime\prime}B^{(2)}(0,\gamma s)\}.

The cone
\Gamma_{\beta}
defined before in (
0.1
) corresponds to the cone
\Gamma_{\beta,\beta^{2}}
above.

Take w-invertible Poisson bounded functions
\varphi^{(1)}
on
\mathbb{H}^{\nu}
and
\varphi^{(2)}
on
\mathbb{R}
, both with mean
0
and with w-inverses with mean
0
.
As usual we write
\varphi^{(1)}_{r}
and
\varphi^{(2)}_{s}
for their normalised dilates, and
\varphi_{r,s}
for
\varphi^{(1)}_{r}\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(2)}_{s}
.
The normalised characteristic functions
|B^{(1)}(o,r)|^{-1}\mathbbm{1}_{B^{(1)}(o,r)}
and
|B^{(2)}(0,s)|^{-1}\mathbbm{1}_{B^{(2)}(0,s)}
are denoted by
\chi^{(1)}_{r}
and
\chi^{(2)}_{s}
, and
\chi_{r,s}=\chi^{(1)}_{r}\ast_{(1)}\chi^{(2)}_{s}
.

Definition 3.2
.

Suppose that
\varphi^{(1)}_{r}
,
\varphi^{(2)}_{s}
,
\chi^{(1)}_{r}
and
\chi^{(2)}_{s}
are as described above, and that
\beta,\gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
.
For
f\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, we define the
Lusin–Littlewood–Paley area function

\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi},\beta,\gamma}(f)
associated to
\varphi^{(1)}
and
\varphi^{(2)}
by

(3.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi},\beta,\gamma}(f)(g):=\biggl(\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}\bigl|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}\bigr|^{2}\ast_{(1)}\chi_{\beta r,\gamma s}(g)\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\frac{ds}{s}\biggr)^{1/2}

for all
g\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu}
, and we define the Hardy space
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi},\beta,\gamma}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
to be the set of all
f\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
for which
\left\|\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi},\beta,\gamma}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}<\infty
, with seminorm

(3.2)

\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi},\beta,\gamma}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}:=\left\|\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi},\beta,\gamma}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}.

In Section
1.8
, for all continuous
F:\mathbb{H}^{\nu}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\to[0,+\infty)
, we defined

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{\beta,\gamma}(F)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}}\left(\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}(F(\cdot,r,s)\ast_{(1)}\chi^{(1)}_{\beta r}\ast_{(2)}\chi^{(2)}_{\gamma s})(g)\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\frac{ds}{s}\right)^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}g;

\displaystyle\mathrm{J}_{\beta,\gamma}(F)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}}\left(\iiint_{\Gamma_{\beta,\gamma}(g)}\frac{F(g^{\prime},r,s)}{\left|T(o,\beta r,\gamma s)\right|}\,\mathrm{d}g^{\prime}\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\frac{ds}{s}\right)^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}g.

In Lemma
1.36
, we showed that
\mathrm{I}_{\beta,\gamma}(F)\eqsim_{\beta,\gamma,\beta^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime}}\mathrm{I}_{\beta^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}(F)
and
\mathrm{I}_{\beta,\gamma/2}(F)\lesssim_{\beta,\gamma}\mathrm{J}_{\beta,\gamma}(F)\lesssim_{\beta,\gamma}\mathrm{I}_{\beta,\gamma}(F)
.
This shows that the Hardy space
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi},\beta,\gamma}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
does not depend on the parameters
\beta
and
\gamma
.
Usually we take these parameters to be
1
unless explicitly stated otherwise, and write
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
.
Further, from the results of Sections
2.5
(above) and
3.2
(below), it follows that the Hardy space does not depend on
\boldsymbol{\varphi}
either (as long as
\boldsymbol{\varphi}
is w-invertible), and so we may also write
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
.

Lemma
1.36
also implies that there is an equivalent definition of the area integral.

Corollary 3.3
.

The space
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
is the space of all functions
f\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
for which
\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}^{\prime}(f)\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, where

\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}^{\prime}(f)(g)=\biggl(\iiint_{\Gamma(g)}\frac{1}{\left|T(o,r^{\prime},s^{\prime})\right|}\bigl|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r^{\prime},s^{\prime}}(g^{\prime})\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}g^{\prime}\,\frac{dr^{\prime}}{r^{\prime}}\,\frac{ds^{\prime}}{s^{\prime}}\biggr)^{1/2},

and
\left\|\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}^{\prime}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}
is equivalent to the
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
seminorm.

If
f
is a polynomial of low degree, then
f\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)}_{r}\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{s}=0
for all
r,s\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, and for such
f
, it is clear that
\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}=0
.
Thus it is reasonable to ask whether
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}
really is a norm.
The answer is given by the next two results.

Proposition 3.4
.

Suppose that
f\in\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
and that
\boldsymbol{\varphi}
is w-invertible.
Then

\left\|\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\eqsim\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}.

Proof.

Since
\chi^{(1)}_{r}
and
\chi^{(2)}_{s}
are normalised characteristic functions,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}\bigl|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}\bigr|^{2}\ast_{(1)}\chi_{r,s}(g)\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}\bigl|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}\bigr|^{2}\ast_{(1)}\chi^{(1)}_{r}\ast_{(2)}\chi^{(2)}_{s}(g)\,\mathrm{d}g\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\frac{ds}{s}

\displaystyle=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}}\bigl|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g)\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}g\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\frac{ds}{s}

\displaystyle\eqsim\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}^{2},

by Corollary
1.45
.
∎

Lemma 3.5
.

Left translations act isometrically and continuously on
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
.
Further,
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}
is a norm on
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
.

Proof.

Recall that
{}_{g}f
denotes the left translate of
f
by
g
.
If
\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}
is finite, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}({}_{g}f)={}_{g}\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\qquad\forall g\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu}\quad\forall f\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu}),

hence the
\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
norms of
\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}({}_{g}f)
and
\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)
coincide; further,

\lim_{g\to o}\left\|{}_{g}f-f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}=0

by Lemma
1.37
.

Take an approximate identity for convolution
(\psi_{n})
of
\mathsf{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
functions with supports shrinking to
o
in
\mathbb{H}^{\nu}
.
If
f\in\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, then, by sublinearity,
\psi_{n}\ast_{(1)}f\in\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
,

(3.3)

\left\|\psi_{n}\ast_{(1)}f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\leq\left\|\psi_{n}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}.

If
f\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
and
\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}=0
, then
\psi_{n}\ast_{(1)}f\in\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\cap\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, and
\left\|\psi_{n}\ast_{(1)}f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}=0
from (
3.3
), so
\psi_{n}\ast_{(1)}f=0
in
\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
from Proposition
3.4
.
Thus
\psi_{n}\ast_{(1)}f=0
in
\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
.
Further,
\psi_{n}\ast_{(1)}f\to f
in
\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
as
n\to\infty
so
f=0
in
\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
.
∎

3.2.
The inclusion
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\subseteq\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})

Here we show that every
f\in\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
has an atomic decomposition, and control the decomposition appropriately, provided
\varphi^{(1)}
and
\varphi^{(2)}
satisfy appropriate conditions.
To be more specific, we will show the following result.

Theorem 3.6
.

Suppose that
M,N\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, and that
\varphi^{(1)}
and
\varphi^{(2)}
are Poisson bounded, as in Definition
1.26
, and w-invertible as in Definition
1.43
, with w-inverses
\psi^{(1)}
and
\psi^{(2)}
of the form
\mathcal{L}_{(1)}^{M}\breve{\psi}^{(1)}
and
\mathcal{L}_{(2)}^{N}\breve{\psi}^{(2)}
.
Then there is a constant
C
, depending on
\nu
,
\boldsymbol{\varphi}
and
\boldsymbol{\psi}
, such that for all
f\in\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, there exist numbers
\lambda_{j}
and
(1,2,M,N,3)
atoms
a_{j}
, for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
, such that
f\sim\sum\lambda_{j}a_{j}
, and

\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\leq\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}|\lambda_{j}|\leq C\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}.

Hence
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\subseteq\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
.

Proof.

From Lemma
3.5
, without loss of generality, we may restrict attention to
f
in
\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\cap\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
.

We denote by
\mathfrak{R}
the collection of all shards, as in Section
1.2
.
We take the enlargement parameter
\kappa
(defined at the start of Section
2
) to be
3
, and then (
1.15
) implies that

\displaystyle RB^{(1)}(o,q)B^{(2)}(0,h)

\displaystyle\subset g\bar{B}^{(1)}(o,q/2)\bar{B}^{(2)}(0,(q^{2}+4h)/8)B^{(1)}(o,q)B^{(2)}(0,h)

\displaystyle=gB^{(1)}(o,3q/2)B^{(2)}(0,(q^{2}+12h)/8)

\displaystyle\subseteq gB^{(1)}(o,\kappa q/2)B^{(2)}(0,\kappa^{2}(q^{2}+4h)/8)

\displaystyle=R^{*},

when
R\in\mathfrak{R}
,
\operatorname{c}(R)=g
,
\operatorname{w}(R)=q
and
\operatorname{h}(R)=h
.
By Lemma
1.12
, for all open sets
E
and
R\in\mathfrak{R}(E)
,

R^{*}\subseteq\left\{g\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu}:\mathcal{M}_{F}(\mathbbm{1}_{E})(g)>\frac{1}{2^{2\nu}\kappa^{\delta}(5\nu+2)}\right\}.

For each tile
R
in
\mathfrak{R}
, the tent
\mathrm{T}(R)
over
R
is defined by

\mathrm{T}(R):=\left\{(g,r,s)\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}:g\in R,r\in(q/(2\nu+1),q],s\in(0,q^{2}]\right\},

where
q=\operatorname{w}(R)
, and for each shard
R
in
\mathfrak{R}
that is not a tile, the tent
\mathrm{T}(R)
over
R
is defined by

\mathrm{T}(R):=\left\{(g,r,s)\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}:g\in R,r\in(q/(2\nu+1),q],s\in(h/(2\nu+1)^{2},h]\right\},

where
q=\operatorname{w}(R)
and
h=\operatorname{h}(R)
.
It is evident that
\mathbb{H}^{\nu}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
decomposes as a disjoint union:

\mathbb{H}^{\nu}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}=\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}R\in\mathfrak{R}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{T}(R);

indeed, for
(g,r,s)\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
, the coordinates
r
and
s
determine the dimensions and the coordinate
g
determines the location of a shard
R
such that
g\in R
.

Take
f\in\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\cap\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
.
For each
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
, we define

\displaystyle E_{\ell}

\displaystyle:=\left\{g\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu}:\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)(g)>2^{\ell}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}

\displaystyle:=\left\{R\in\mathfrak{R}:|R^{*}\cap E_{\ell+1}|\leq\frac{1}{3\kappa^{\delta}}|R^{*}|<|R^{*}\cap E_{\ell}|\right\}

\displaystyle\tilde{E}_{\ell}

\displaystyle:=\left\{g\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu}:\mathcal{M}_{F}(\mathbbm{1}_{E_{\ell}})(g)>\frac{1}{2^{2\nu}\kappa^{\delta}(5\nu+2)}\right\}.

The reader may check that if
R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}
and
(g,r,s)\in\mathrm{T}(R)
, then
R^{*}\subseteq\tilde{E}_{\ell}
and

(3.4)

\frac{1}{3\kappa^{\delta}}|R^{*}|\leq\frac{1}{2}|T(g,r,s)|,

The hypothesis on the w-invertibility of
\varphi^{(1)}
and
\varphi^{(2)}
implies that

f=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}\ast_{(1)}\psi_{r,s}\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\frac{dr}{r}\,.

For the moment, we assume also that
\psi^{(1)}
and
\psi^{(2)}
are supported in
B^{(1)}(o,1)
and
B^{(2)}(0,1)
.
At the end of the proof, we remove this support assumption.

As before, we write
{}_{g}\psi_{r,s}
for the left translate
g^{\prime}\mapsto\psi_{r,s}(g^{-1}g^{\prime})
.
It follows from the reproducing formula above that for all
f\in\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\cap\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
,

(3.5)

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g)\,{}_{g}\psi_{r,s}\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{R\in\mathcal{B}_{\ell}}\iiint_{\mathrm{T}(R)}f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g)\,{}_{g}\psi_{r,s}\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\lambda_{\ell}\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}a_{\ell,R},

say, where

\displaystyle a_{\ell,R}:=\frac{1}{\lambda_{\ell}}\iiint_{\mathrm{T}(R)}f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g)\,{}_{g}\psi_{r,s}\,\mathrm{d}g\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\frac{ds}{s}

\displaystyle\lambda_{\ell}:=\bigg\|\biggl(\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}\left|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(\cdot)\right|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{T}(R)}(\cdot)\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\frac{ds}{s}\biggr)^{1/2}\bigg\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}|\tilde{E}_{\ell}|^{1/2}.

(If
\lambda_{\ell}=0
, then all
a_{\ell,R}
are taken to be
0
, as the integral involved in the definition of
a_{\ell,R}
vanishes.
At the end of this proof, in (
3.8
) we show that
\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\lambda_{\ell}
is finite, and so these expressions make sense.)

Fix
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
; we claim that
\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}a_{\ell,R}
is a geometric multiple of a flag atom.
From Lemma
2.4
, it suffices to prove that there exist functions
b_{\ell,R}
in
\operatorname{Dom}(\mathcal{L}_{(1)}^{M}\mathcal{L}_{(2)}^{N})
, for all
R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}
, such that

(A1)

a_{\ell,R}=\mathcal{L}_{(1)}^{M}\mathcal{L}_{(2)}^{N}b_{R}
and
\operatorname{supp}b_{R}\subseteq R^{*}
; and

(A2)

for all sign sequences
\sigma:\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}\to\{\pm 1\}
, the sum
\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}\sigma_{\ell,R}\,a_{\ell,R}
converges in
\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, to
a_{\sigma}
say, and

\left\|a_{\sigma}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\lesssim\left|E\right|^{-1/2}
.

Suppose that
\operatorname{w}(R)=q
and
\operatorname{h}(R)=h
.
Evidently, for each
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
and
R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}
,

a_{\ell,R}:=\mathcal{L}_{(1)}^{M}\mathcal{L}_{(2)}^{N}b_{\ell,R},

where

(3.6)

\displaystyle b_{\ell,R}:=\frac{1}{\lambda_{\ell}}\iiint_{\mathrm{T}(R)}f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g)\,{}_{g}\breve{\psi}_{r,s}\,\mathrm{d}g\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\frac{ds}{s}\,.

By construction, when
(g,r,s)\in\mathrm{T}(R)
,
\operatorname{supp}{}_{g}\breve{\psi}_{r,s}\subseteq gT(o,r,s)\subseteq RT(o,q,h)
, so

(3.7)

\displaystyle\operatorname{supp}b_{\ell,R}\subseteq R^{*}.

Our next step is to take a sign sequence
\sigma:\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}\to\{\pm 1\}
and estimate the
\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
norm of
\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}\sigma_{\ell,R}\,a_{\ell,R}
.
For all
h\in\mathsf{C}_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
such that
\left\|h\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\Bigl|\int_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}}\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}\sigma_{\ell,R}\,a_{\ell,R}(g^{\prime})h(g^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}g^{\prime}\Bigr|

\displaystyle\qquad=\Biggl|\frac{1}{\lambda_{\ell}}\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}\sigma_{\ell,R}\iiint_{\mathrm{T}(R)}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}}f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g){}_{g}\psi_{r,s}(g^{\prime})h(g^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}g^{\prime}\,\frac{ds}{s}\frac{dr}{r}\,\mathrm{d}g\Biggr|

\displaystyle\qquad=\Biggl|\frac{1}{\lambda_{\ell}}\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}\sigma_{\ell,R}\iiint_{\mathrm{T}(R)}h\ast_{(1)}\mathcal{R}\psi_{r,s}(g)f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g)\,\frac{ds}{s}\frac{dr}{r}\,\mathrm{d}g\Biggr|

\displaystyle\qquad=\Biggl|\frac{1}{\lambda_{\ell}}\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}\sigma_{\ell,R}\iiint_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}h\ast_{(1)}\mathcal{R}\psi_{r,s}(g)f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{T}(R)}(g,r,s)\,\frac{ds}{s}\frac{dr}{r}\,\mathrm{d}g\Biggr|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{\lambda_{\ell}}\biggl(\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}\iiint_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}|h\ast_{(1)}\mathcal{R}\psi_{r,s}(g)|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{T}(R)}(g,r,s)\,\frac{ds}{s}\frac{dr}{r}\,\mathrm{d}g\biggr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\biggl(\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}\iiint_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}\left|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g)\right|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{T}(R)}(g,r,s)\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\mathrm{d}g\biggr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{\lambda_{\ell}}\biggl(\iiint_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}|h\ast_{(1)}\mathcal{R}\psi_{r,s}(g)|^{2}\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\mathrm{d}g\biggr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\biggl(\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}\iiint_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}\left|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g)\right|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{T}(R)}(g,r,s)\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\mathrm{d}g\biggr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda_{\ell}}\left\|h\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\left\|\biggl(\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}\left|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(\cdot)\right|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{T}(R)}(\cdot,r,s)\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\frac{dr}{r}\biggr)^{1/2}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq|\tilde{E}_{\ell}|^{-{1}/{2}},

by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, the square function estimate of Corollary
1.45
and the definition of
\lambda_{\ell}
.
It follows that
\left\|\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}\sigma_{\ell,R}\,a_{\ell,R}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\lesssim|\tilde{E}_{\ell}|^{-1/2}
, whence
a
is a multiple of an atom, and the multiple depends only on
M
,
N
,
\nu
and
\boldsymbol{\varphi}
.

Finally, we verify the convergence of the series
\sum_{\ell}|\lambda_{\ell}|
.
To do this, we first fix
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
, and observe that if
R\in\mathfrak{R}
and
(g^{\prime},r,s)\in\mathrm{T}(R)
, then, from (
1.15
),

T(g^{\prime},r,s)\subseteq R^{*}\subseteq\tilde{E}_{\ell}
, whence

\left|\tilde{E}_{\ell}\cap T(g^{\prime},r,s)\right|=\left|T(g^{\prime},r,s)\right|=\left|T(o,r,s)\right|,

while, by definition of
\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}
and (
3.4
),

\left|E_{\ell+1}\cap T(g^{\prime},r,s)\right|\leq\left|R^{*}\cap E_{\ell+1}\right|\leq\frac{1}{3\kappa^{\delta}}\left|R^{*}\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}\left|T(o,r,s)\right|.

It follows that

\frac{\left|(\tilde{E}_{\ell}\setminus E_{\ell+1})\cap T(g^{\prime},r,s)\right|}{\left|T(g^{\prime},r,s)\right|}\geq\frac{1}{2}\,.

Now by the definitions of
\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}
, the Lusin–Littlewood–Paley area function, and
E_{\ell}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}\iiint_{\mathrm{T}(R)}\left|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g^{\prime})\right|^{2}\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\mathrm{d}g^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}\iiint_{\mathrm{T}(R)}\left|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g^{\prime})\right|^{2}\frac{\left|(\tilde{E}_{\ell}\setminus E_{\ell+1})\cap T(g^{\prime},r,s)\right|}{\left|T(g^{\prime},r,s)\right|}\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\mathrm{d}g^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2\iiint_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}\left|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g^{\prime})\right|^{2}\frac{\left|(\tilde{E}_{\ell}\setminus E_{\ell+1})\cap T(g^{\prime},r,s)\right|}{\left|T(g^{\prime},r,s)\right|}\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\mathrm{d}g^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad=2\int_{\tilde{E}_{\ell}\backslash E_{\ell+1}}\iiint_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}\left|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g^{\prime})\right|^{2}\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{T(o,r,s)}(g^{\prime}g^{-1})}{\left|T(o,r,s)\right|}\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\mathrm{d}g^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle\qquad=2\int_{\tilde{E}_{\ell}\backslash E_{\ell+1}}\iiint_{\Gamma(g)}\frac{1}{\left|T(o,r,s)\right|}\left|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g^{\prime})\right|^{2}\frac{1}{\left|T(o,r,s)\right|}\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\mathrm{d}g^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle\qquad=2\int_{\tilde{E}_{\ell}\backslash E_{\ell+1}}\left|\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)(g)\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2^{2\ell+3}|\tilde{E}_{\ell}|.

Hence

(3.8)

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}|\lambda_{\ell}|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\left\|\biggl(\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}\left|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(\cdot)\right|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{T}(R)}(\cdot,r,s)\,\frac{dr}{r}\frac{ds}{s}\biggr)^{1/2}\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}

\displaystyle\times\left|\tilde{E}_{\ell}\right|^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\biggl(\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}\iiint_{\mathrm{T}(R)}\left|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g^{\prime})\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}g^{\prime}\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\frac{ds}{s}\biggr)^{1/2}|\tilde{E}_{\ell}|^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{\ell+3/2}|\tilde{E}_{\ell}|\lesssim\left\|\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\psi}}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}

\displaystyle=\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}.

It remains to remove an assumption that we made earlier in the proof.
If
\breve{\psi}^{(1)}
is not supported in
B^{(1)}(o,1)
, we use Lemma
1.30
to write
\breve{\psi}^{(1)}
as a sum
\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}[\breve{\psi}^{(1,i)}]_{2^{i}}

of dilates of functions
\breve{\psi}^{(1,i)}
that are supported in
B^{(1)}(o,1)
and such that
\left\|\breve{\psi}^{(1,i)}\right\|_{(Q)}\lesssim 2^{-i}\left\|\breve{\psi}^{(1)}\right\|_{(Q)}
; we treat
\breve{\psi}^{(2)}
similarly.

Then we replace (
3.5
) by

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g)\sum_{i,j\in\mathbb{N}}{}_{g}\psi^{(i,j)}_{2^{i}r,2^{j}s}\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{R\in\mathcal{B}_{\ell}}\iiint_{\mathrm{T}(R)}f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{2^{-i}r,2^{-j}s}(g)\,{}_{g}\psi^{(i,j)}_{r,s}\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\frac{ds}{s}\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\lambda^{(i,j)}_{\ell}\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}a^{(i,j)}_{\ell,R},

and treat each

\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\lambda^{(i,j)}_{\ell}\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}a^{(i,j)}_{\ell,R}

much as we treated

\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\lambda_{\ell}\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}a_{\ell,R}
before.

This completes the proof of Theorem
3.6
.
∎

We remark that, when
f\in\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, the atomic decomposition
\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\lambda_{\ell}a_{\ell}
in the proof above also converges to
f
in the
\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
norm.
To prove this, we only need to show that
\|\sum_{|\ell|>L}\lambda_{\ell}a_{\ell}\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\rightarrow 0
as
L
tends to infinity.
For this, first note that

\displaystyle\Bigl|\big\langle\sum_{|\ell|>L}\lambda_{\ell}a_{\ell},h\big\rangle\Bigr|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\biggl|\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}\iiint_{\mathrm{T}(R)}h\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g^{\prime})\sum_{|\ell|>L}\lambda_{\ell}a_{\ell}\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}g^{\prime}\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\frac{ds}{s}\biggr|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\int_{\mathbb{H}^{\nu}}\biggl(\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}\left|h\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g^{\prime})\right|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{T}(R)}(g^{\prime},r,s)\,\frac{dr}{r}\frac{ds}{s}\biggr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\biggl(\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}\biggl|\sum_{|\ell|>L}\lambda_{\ell}a_{\ell}\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g^{\prime})\biggr|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{T}(R)}(g^{\prime},r,s)\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\frac{ds}{s}\biggr)^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}g^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\left\|h\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\bigg\|\biggl(\sum_{R\in\mathfrak{R}_{\ell}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}\biggl|\sum_{|\ell|>L}\lambda_{\ell}a_{\ell}\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g^{\prime})\biggr|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{T}(R)}(g^{\prime},r,s)\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\frac{ds}{s}\biggr)^{1/2}\bigg\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}

\displaystyle\qquad\to 0

when
L
tends to
\infty
, as the area integral operator is bounded on
\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
.
Since

\biggl\|\sum_{|\ell|>L}\lambda_{\ell}a_{\ell}\biggr\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}=\sup_{h:\left\|h\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})=1}}\Bigl|\big\langle\sum_{|\ell|>L}\lambda_{\ell}a_{\ell},h\big\rangle\Bigr|,

the sum
\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\lambda_{\ell}a_{\ell}
converges to
f
in the
\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
norm.

Finally, we observe that the same method also proves the following result.

Theorem 3.7
.

Suppose that
M,N\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, and that
\varphi^{(1)}
and
\varphi^{(2)}
are Poisson bounded, as in Definition
1.26
, and w-invertible as in Definition
1.43
, with w-inverses
\psi^{(1)}
and
\psi^{(2)}
of the form
\mathcal{D}\breve{\psi}^{(1)}
and
\mathcal{T}^{2N}\breve{\psi}^{(2)}
, where
\mathcal{D}
is the
2M
-fold product tensor
\nabla_{(1)}\otimes\dots\otimes\nabla_{(1)}
and
\breve{\varphi}^{(1)}
is a dual tensor.
Then there is a constant
C
, depending on
\nu
and
\boldsymbol{\varphi}
, such that for all
f\in\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, there exist numbers
\lambda_{j}
and
(1,2,M,N,3)
moments atoms
a_{j}
, for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
, such that
f\sim\sum\lambda_{j}a_{j}
, and

\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{momat}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\leq\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}|\lambda_{j}|\leq C\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}.

Hence
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\subseteq\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{momat}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
.

The point of this is that we may be able to show that
\varphi^{(1)}
has a w-inverse of the form in this theorem, but not of the form in Theorem
3.6
.
However, if
f\in\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{momat}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, then
f\in\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, where
\varphi^{(1)}\in\mathsf{A}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, the space mentioned at the start of Section
1.9
, and for such
\boldsymbol{\varphi}
, it is possible to find a w-inverse of the form in
3.6
, and it follows that
f\in\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
.
Hence
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{momat}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
and
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
coincide.

3.3.
Remarks on a “discrete area function”

We could replace the integrals over
r
and
s
in the definition of the area function
\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}
(Definition
3.2
) by sums, taking
r
and
s
to be
2^{j}
and
2^{k}
, or more generally
\alpha^{j}
and
\beta^{k}
.

The proofs that
\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
if
f\in\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
in Section
2.5
and that
f\in\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
if
\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{area},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
in Section
3.2
go through with minor modifications, and show that the atomic Hardy space may also be characterised by a “discrete area function”.
We spare the reader the details!

4.
The square function Hardy space

We recall the definitions of the continuous and discrete Littlewood–Paley square functions.
As usual, given functions
\varphi^{(1)}
on
\mathbb{H}^{\nu}
and
\varphi^{(2)}
on
\mathbb{R}
, we write
\varphi^{(1)}_{r}
and
\varphi^{(2)}_{s}
for their normalised dilates, and
\varphi_{r,s}
for
\varphi^{(1)}_{r}\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{s}
.

Definition 4.1
.

Suppose that
\varphi^{(1)}
and
\varphi^{(2)}
have mean
0
and are Poisson-bounded, as in Definition
1.26
.
Suppose also that
\boldsymbol{\varphi}
is w-invertible (continuously or discretely, according to the Hardy space that we are going to define).
For
f\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, we define the
continuous and discrete Littlewood–Paley square functions

\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{cts},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)
and
\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{dis},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)
associated to
\varphi^{(1)}
and
\varphi^{(2)}
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{cts},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)(g):=\biggl(\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}}\bigl|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)}_{r}\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{s}(g)\bigr|^{2}\,\frac{dr}{r}\,\frac{ds}{s}\biggr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{dis},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)(g):=\biggl(\sum_{(m,n)\in\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}}\bigl|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi^{(1)}_{2^{m}}\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{2^{n}}(g)\bigr|^{2}\biggr)^{1/2}

for all
g\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu}
.
We define the
square function Hardy spaces

\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{cts},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
and
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{dis},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, often abbreviated to
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{cts}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
and
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{dis}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
, to be the set of all
f\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
for which
\left\|\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{cts},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}<\infty
or
\left\|\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{dis},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}<\infty
, with norms

\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{cts},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}:=\left\|\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{cts},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}

and

\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{dis},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}:=\left\|\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{dis},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}.

We remark that
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{cts},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}
and
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{dis},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}
are indeed norms, for much the same reason as
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}
is a norm (see the beginning of Section
3
).

Han, Lu and Sawyer studied the square function Hardy spaces (but using left convolutions rather than right convolutions), and showed that these do not depend on the choice of
\boldsymbol{\varphi}
.
However, it is not clear to us that their techniques cover all the pairs
\boldsymbol{\varphi}
that we consider, as at least some of their arguments assume that
\varphi^{(1)}
lies in the space
\mathsf{A}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
mentioned in Section
1.11
.

We showed in Lemma
1.47
that every continuous square function
\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{cts},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)
dominates a discrete square function
\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{cts},\boldsymbol{\psi}}(f)
, and it follows that
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{cts},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\subseteq\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{dis},\boldsymbol{\psi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
.
In Section
2
we established that
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\subseteq\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{cts}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
and
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\subseteq\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{dis}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
.
We may modify the proof that all
f
in the area function Hardy space admit atomic decompositions to show that if
\mathcal{S}_{F,\mathrm{dis},\boldsymbol{\psi}}(f)
is integrable then
f
has an atomic decomposition.
Hence
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{cts},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\subseteq\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{dis},\boldsymbol{\psi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\subseteq\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
.

5.
The maximal function Hardy spaces

In this section, we characterise the flag Hardy space by maximal functions.
There are two aspects of this.
First, we show that we obtain the same Hardy space irrespective of whether we define it using a radial, nontangential or grand maximal function.
Then we show that the Hardy space defined using the nontangential maximal function associated to the Poisson kernel is a subspace of the area function Hardy space.
More precisely, by combining the results of Sections
3
and
2
with some obvious inclusions, we know that

\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\subseteq\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{atom}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\subseteq\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{gmax}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\subseteq\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{nontan}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\subseteq\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{radial}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu}).

We complete the identification of these five spaces by showing that

\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{radial}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\subseteq\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{nontan}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{nontan}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})\subseteq\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{area}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu}).

All inclusions are continuous, with corresponding norm inequalities.

We begin by recalling some relevant definitions.
Take Poisson bounded functions
\varphi^{(1)}
on
\mathbb{H}^{\nu}
and
\varphi^{(2)}
on
\mathbb{R}
both of which have total integral
1
, and write
\varphi_{r,s}
for the convolution
\varphi^{(1)}_{r}\ast_{(2)}\varphi^{(2)}_{s}
of their normalised dilates.

Definition 5.1
.

Define the
radial maximal operator
associated to
\boldsymbol{\varphi}
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{radial},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)(g):=\sup_{r,s\in\mathbb{R}^{+}}|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g)|\qquad\forall g\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu}\quad\forall f\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu}),

and let
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{radial},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
be the set of all
f\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
such that
\left\|\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{radial},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}
is finite, with norm

(5.1)

\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{radial}}\varphi(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}:=\left\|\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{radial},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}.

Recall that
\Gamma_{\beta,\gamma}(g):=\{(g^{\prime},r,s)\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}:g^{\prime}\in gB^{(1)}(o,\beta r)B^{(2)}(0,\gamma s)\}
for all
\beta,\gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
.

Definition 5.2
.

Define the
nontangential maximal operator
associated to
\beta
,
\gamma
and
\boldsymbol{\varphi}
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{*}_{F,\boldsymbol{\varphi},\beta,\gamma}(f)(g)=\sup\{\left|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g^{\prime})\right|:(g^{\prime},r,s)\in\Gamma_{\beta,\gamma}(g)\}

for all
g\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu}
and all
f\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
,
and let
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{nontan},\boldsymbol{\varphi},\beta,\gamma}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
be the linear space of all

f\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
such that
\left\|\mathcal{M}^{*}_{F,\boldsymbol{\varphi},\beta,\gamma}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}
is finite, with norm

(5.2)

\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{nontan},\boldsymbol{\varphi},\beta,\gamma}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}:=\left\|\mathcal{M}^{*}_{F,\boldsymbol{\varphi},\beta,\gamma}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}.

We are going to see shortly that this space is independent of
\beta
and
\gamma
, so we usually take both these to be
1
and omit the corresponding suffices.
Later we show that
\boldsymbol{\varphi}
is also irrelevant.

Definition 5.3
.

Let
\mathsf{F}
be a Poisson bounded family of pairs of functions, as in Definition
1.26
, which contains at least one pair of functions
(\varphi^{(1)},\varphi^{(2)})
such that
\int_{G}\varphi^{(1)}(g)\,\mathrm{d}g=1
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi^{(2)}(g_{2})\,\mathrm{d}g_{2}=1
.
Define the
grand maximal operator
associated to
\mathsf{F}
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}}(f)(g):=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}\in\mathsf{F}}\sup_{r,s\in\mathbb{R}^{+}}\left|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g)\right|\qquad\forall g\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu}\quad\forall f\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu}),

and
\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{gmax}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
to be the space of all
f\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
such that
\left\|\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}
is finite, with norm

(5.3)

\left\|f\right\|_{\mathsf{H}^{1}_{F,\mathrm{gmax}}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}:=\left\|\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}.

If we take a “large enough” family
\mathsf{F}
, the pairs of heat kernels
(h^{(1)}_{1},h^{(2)}_{1})
and Poisson kernels
(p^{(1)}_{1},p^{(2)}_{1})
and all their translates by elements of a bounded subsets of
\mathbb{H}^{\nu}
and of
\mathbb{R}
belong to
\mathsf{F}
.

It is clear that the grand maximal operator dominates the nontangential maximal operator, which in turn dominates the radial maximal operator.
Further, we may dominate
\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}}
by the flag maximal operator
\mathcal{M}_{F}
(see Section
1.6
), so
\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}}
is
\mathsf{L}^{p}
bounded for all
p\in(1,\infty]
.

We check that we are dealing with norms.
It is obvious that the expressions defined in (
5.1
), (
5.2
) and (
5.3
) are seminorms.
If
f\in\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
and
\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{radial},\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)=0
, then
f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}=0
for all
r,s\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
.
Now
f=\lim_{r,s\to 0}f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}
in
\mathsf{L}^{1}(G)
, so
f=0
by a standard approximate identity argument.

5.1.
The tangential maximal function

We begin our study of maximal functions by showing that the behaviour of the nontangential maximal function is to a degree independent of the apertures
\beta
and
\gamma
of the cone in its definition.
We take a continuous function
F:\mathbb{H}^{\nu}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\times R^{+}\to[0,\infty)
, and define

F_{\alpha,\beta}(g)=\sup\{F(g^{\prime},r,s):g^{\prime}\in T(g,\alpha r,\beta s)\}.

Then Lemma
1.35
shows that, for all
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime},\beta,\beta^{\prime},\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
such that
\varepsilon
is small and
\alpha<\alpha^{\prime}
and
\beta<\beta^{\prime}
,

(5.4)

\displaystyle\|F_{\alpha,\beta}\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\leq\|F_{\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}\max\left\{\left(\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\alpha}\right)^{\delta},\left(\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\alpha}\right)^{2\nu}\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta}\right\}^{1+\varepsilon}\|F_{\alpha,\beta}\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}.

This shows that changing the apertures
\beta
and
\gamma
of the flag cone in the definition of the nontangential maximal function does not change the Hardy space that arises.

This lemma enables us to control the
tangential maximal operator

\mathcal{M}^{**}_{F,\boldsymbol{\varphi}}
, defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{**}_{F,\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)(g)

\displaystyle=\sup\Bigl\{\left|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(gg_{1}g_{2}))\right|\Bigl(1+\frac{\left\|g_{1}\right\|}{r}\Bigr)^{-\delta-\varepsilon}\Bigl(1+\frac{1+\left\|g_{2}\right\|}{s}\Bigr)^{-1-\varepsilon}:

\displaystyle r,s\in\mathbb{R}^{+},g_{1}\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu},g_{2}\in\mathbb{R}\Bigr\};

here
\varepsilon
is small and positive.
Note that, given
g_{3}\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu}
, there are infinitely many
g_{1}\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu}
and
g_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
g_{3}=g_{1}g_{2}
.
Of these,
(1+\left\|g_{1}\right\|/r)^{-\delta-\varepsilon}(1+\left\|g_{2}\right\|/s)^{-1-\varepsilon}
is maximal when
\left\|g_{1}\right\|
is minimal.
As noted earlier, in our discussion of the geometry of tubes, if
g_{3}
is written as
(z,t)
, then this happens when
\left\|g_{1}\right\|=|z|_{\infty}
and
|g_{2}|=0
if
|t|\leq|z|_{\infty}^{2}
and
|g_{2}|=|t|-|z|_{\infty}^{2}
otherwise.

Corollary 5.4
.

The following inequality holds:

\left\|\mathcal{M}^{**}_{F,\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\lesssim\left\|\mathcal{M}^{*}_{F,\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}.

Proof.

Observe that
\mathbb{H}^{\nu}=\bigcup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}A^{(1,i)}
and
\mathbb{R}=\bigcup_{j\in\mathbb{N}}A^{(2,j)}
, where

\displaystyle A^{(1,0)}=B^{(1)}(o,1)

\displaystyle\qquad\text{and}\qquad A^{(1,i)}=B^{(1)}(o,2^{i})\setminus B^{(1)}(o,2^{i-1})\qquad\forall i\in\mathbb{N}^{+}

and

\displaystyle A^{(2,0)}=B^{(2)}(0,1)

\displaystyle\qquad\text{and}\qquad A^{(2,j)}=B^{(2)}(0,2^{j})\setminus B^{(2)}(0,2^{j-1})\qquad\forall j\in\mathbb{N}^{+}.

Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{**}_{F,\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)(g)

\displaystyle\leq\sup\Bigl\{\left|f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(gg_{1}g_{2}))\right|\Bigl(1+\frac{\left\|g_{1}\right\|}{r}\Bigr)^{-\delta-\varepsilon}\Bigl(1+\frac{\left\|g_{2}\right\|}{s}\Bigr)^{-1-\varepsilon}:

\displaystyle\qquad g_{1}\in A^{(1,i)}(r),g_{2}\in A^{(2,j)}(s),i,j\in\mathbb{N},r,s\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\sup\Bigl\{\mathcal{M}^{*}_{F,\boldsymbol{\varphi},2^{i},2^{j}}(g)\bigl(1+2^{i-1}\bigr)^{-\delta-\varepsilon}\bigl(1+2^{j-1}\bigr)^{-1-\varepsilon}:i,j\in\mathbb{N},r,s\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i,j\in\mathbb{N}}2^{-i(\delta+\varepsilon)-j(1+\varepsilon)}\mathcal{M}^{*}_{F,\boldsymbol{\varphi},2^{i},2^{j}}(f)(g)\qquad\forall g\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu},

so, from (
5.4
), with
F(g,r,s)
taken to be
f\ast_{(1)}\varphi_{r,s}(g)
, and a suitable choice of
\varepsilon
,

\left\|\mathcal{M}^{**}_{F,\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\lesssim\sum_{i,j\in\mathbb{N}}2^{-i(\delta+\varepsilon)-j(1+\varepsilon)}\left\|\mathcal{M}^{*}_{F,\boldsymbol{\varphi},2^{i},2^{j}}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}\left\|\mathcal{M}^{*}_{F,\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)\right\|_{\mathsf{L}^{1}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})},

as required.
∎

To state the next lemma, we need a little more notation.
We write
\mathsf{F}_{00}^{(1)}
for a family of functions in
\mathsf{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
that are supported in
B^{(1)}(o,1)
and have mean zero, and
\mathsf{F}_{00}^{(2)}
for a family of functions in
\mathsf{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
that are supported in
B^{(2)}(0,1)
and have mean zero.

Lemma 5.5
.

Suppose that
\varphi^{(1)}:\mathbb{H}^{\nu}\to\mathbb{C}
and
\varphi^{(2)}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{C}
are Poisson bounded and w-invertible, with w-inverses of the form
\mathcal{D}\tilde{\varphi}^{(1)}
and
\mathcal{T}\tilde{\varphi}^{(2)}
, where
\tilde{\varphi}^{(1)}
and
\tilde{\varphi}^{(2)}
are Poisson bounded and
\mathcal{D}
is a differential operator of homogeneous degree
2\delta+2
.
Suppose also that
\mathsf{F}
is one of
\mathsf{F}_{00}^{(1)}\times\mathsf{F}_{00}^{(2)}
,
\mathsf{F}_{00}^{(1)}\times\{\varphi^{(2)}\}
and
\{\varphi^{(1)}\}\times\mathsf{F}_{00}^{(2)}
.
Then

(5.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{F,\mathrm{gmax},\mathsf{F}}(f)(g)\lesssim\mathcal{M}^{**}_{F,\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(f)(g)\qquad\forall g\in\mathbb{H}^{\nu}

for all
f\in\mathsf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{\nu})
.

Proof.
